# Review log — 0112_fix_credential-secrets-in-a-dedicated-namespace-addable-by-secre

## Standing context

Changelog (compaction pass 1, 2026-10-30): lifted the durable residue of every ledger entry from run 1 (spec rounds 1 to 4, f1, redesign 1) and run 2 (spec round 1). Run 2 reuses round ids, so its entries are cited as `r2:<id>`; run 1 ids carry no prefix. Applied CORRECTS and closures as follows. The probe-ordering claims now read 403 per caller plus no probe per rule-3-refused reference (spec.2.*). The compensating delete, the enumerated removal list, and the id-based compensation guard (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.1.fix-G3.1) are superseded by redesign 1. Tenant erasure by stored reference is superseded by the tenant-hash label delete-collection, ordered after `DeleteByTenant` (r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1). The rule 4 "only through the keyed read" OPENs (spec.4.fix-design-G1.1, r2:spec.1.review-applicability.2) are closed by r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1, which also applies the DEFERRED in r2:spec.1.follow-up-fix.1 asking compaction to drop them. Deleted these closed WATCHOUTs: per-entry predicate, probe oracle, seed omitted from removal paths, duplicated rule 4, initial-setup ordering, the §28.7 row, "caches no value" collision, roleArn drop, runbook steps, item-3 lease drain (answered by r2:spec.1.review-edit-sites.1), rule 3 "after an add", the GitOps wave, "never deletes a Secret it did not write", and the §25 two-branch rotation. Retired UNVERIFIED spec.1.review-reliability.1 (answered by spec.4.review-security.1 and the label design) and UNVERIFIED spec.4.review-performance.1 (answered by r2:spec.1.review-performance.1). Merged duplicate DEFERREDs. Reached the 200-line target.

### Settled

- **Token Service read scope.** SPEC-3 (c) **Token Service secret resolution.** governs every Token Service read of a credential Secret; `ProbeSecretAccess` carries the probe and the gateway keyed read (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Namespace and name-only home.** SPEC-3 (c) rule 1 is the one home for "reads only the credentials namespace" and "names the Secret by name alone"; old **Secret references.** rule 4 is deleted (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **No Secret cache.** SPEC-3 (c) rule 2 forbids a Secret cache serving a read; the lease credential cache stays (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Read counting.** SPEC-3 rule 5 says "Each read is counted", matching SPEC-8 (a) "one per credential Secret read or probe" (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Single carrier today.** The tree's only Token Service Secret Get is `secretprobe.Prober.ProbeSecretAccess`; `AssignCredentials` materializes from in-process pool state (spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Absent Token Service.** That edge case is owned by SPEC-2 **Credentials namespace.** rules 4 and 5; SPEC-3 (c) gets no clause (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **SPEC-13.** Replaces only the consumers clause of the §28.7 `schemas/lenny-tokenservice.proto` row, which stays one physical line; no gate pins that cell (spec.1.fix-G1.1, spec.2.review-applicability.1).
- **Summary wording.** "per-call fail-closed" became "per-read" (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Introduced-reference predicate.** Pool-wide, stated only in SPEC-2 **Registering a Secret by reference.**; defined by the request: "A write whose request submits a `secretRef` the pool does not already carry introduces that reference" (spec.1.fix-G2.1, spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **No secretData probe carve-out.** Rule 6's "no probe on a `secretData` write" sentence and CODE-4 step 4's "Skip the probe" are deleted; the submitted-ref definition makes them redundant (spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **Who introduces.** Only `platform-admin` or the bootstrap seed introduces a `secretRef` (narrowing chosen over per-read tenant-id annotation binding) (f1.human-decisions).
- **Probe ordering.** SPEC-2 (g) opening: a caller **Registering a Secret by reference.** refuses gets `403` with no probe; no probe call for a reference that **Secret references.** rule 3 refuses (spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Probe mechanism.** CODE-3 step 3 probes only for a `platform-admin` principal and excludes `credential.IsManaged` refs; its SPEC-2 citation covers both; step 5 (412) runs after steps 4 and 6 (spec.1.fix-G2.1, spec.2.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Mixed-request precedence.** The spec states no precedence among simultaneous refusals; dropped rule 1 no-probe wording was not filed (spec.3.review-mechanism.1).
- **§24.5 roles.** add-credential and update-credential are `tenant-admin` (own tenant)¹; footnote ¹ "Introducing a `secretRef` requires `platform-admin`" is §24.5's single statement; list and get are `tenant-viewer` (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **SPEC-5.** The §10.2 tenant-admin bullet sentence is deleted; (b) and (c) became (a) and (b); the matrix footnote is §10.2's one statement (spec.1.fix-G2.1, spec.1.fix-design-G2.1).
- **Probe error rows.** SPEC-7 (h) and (i) keep the details key and hint and point to §4.9; SPEC-2 (g) rules 1 and 2 keep only the verdict-to-code mapping and "MUST NOT fail open" (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **Probe error contract home.** Category, `details.missingSecrets`, and `details.secretRef` live in the §15.1 catalog rows; code already emits them (spec.2.review-client-surface.1, r2:spec.1.review-citations.1).
- **DOCS-1 lenny-ctl bullet.** Reads "follows SPEC-11, footnote included" (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **Initial setup.** SPEC-3 (b) step 1 creates only by-reference Secrets; step 2's last sentence orders by-value adds after pool creation with pointers to SPEC-2 (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **Emergency runbook.** SPEC-3 (f) under a renamed SPEC-3 heading edits the runbook under `#### Emergency Credential Revocation`; step (5) names the add endpoint only; step (6) gains `secretData` (spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.fix-design-G4.1).
- **NET-040 callout.** SPEC-6 (b) names users by pointer ("the components whose Egress Allowed cell in the table above names it"), resolving to gateway, Token Service, and lenny-ops (spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.2.review-citations.1).
- **Lifecycle home.** Redesign 1: SPEC-2 (f) **Credential values submitted through the admin API.** rules 3 to 6 are the one home: rule 3 operation table, rule 4 lock placement, rule 5 outcomes and residue table, rule 6 no-writer refusal; rules 1, 2, 7, 8 kept numbers (redesign.1).
- **Citers.** SPEC-7 (b), (c), (e), (f), (g), (j), SPEC-9, Edge cases, CODE-4 steps 3 to 7, CODE-5 steps 1 to 3, tests 7 to 10, and summary bullets cite rule numbers; S14 and the CODE-4 index say "Lenny-written Secret operations" (redesign.1).
- **No compensating delete.** Open decision 1 of the redesign kept at Option A; the owner may still reverse it (redesign.1).
- **Forced seed.** A forced bootstrap seed update is a pool write covered by the rule 3 catch-all row; a delete failure is the existing per-entry `SEED_STORE_ERROR` (exit 2) (spec.1.fix-G3.1, redesign.1).
- **Tenant-hash label.** Lenny-written Secrets carry `lenny.dev/tenant-hash` = first 40 hex of SHA-256 over the tenant id (home: rule 2); the gateway Role gains `deletecollection` (spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **Erasure.** SPEC-9 Phase 4 deletes by that label with one `deletecollection`, run directly after `CredentialPoolStore` deletion; CODE-5 calls `Writer.DeleteTenant` (spec.4.fix-G1.1, r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **No label reservation.** "The gateway never deletes a Secret that it did not write." is deleted; the rule 3 table states what is deleted (r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Rule 4 keyed read.** **Credentials namespace.** rule 4 reads "The gateway reads a stored value for VCS tokens ... through the Token Service keyed read ..., for a reference taken from a pool in the caller's tenant"; "only" is gone (r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **VCS key.** SPEC-2 (e) adds a `github` | `token` row to the §4.9 **Secret shape.** table; the gateway reads key `token` (cmd/lenny-gateway/auxstores.go) (spec.4.fix-G2.1).
- **GitOps ordering.** SPEC-3 (e) replaces the whole **Ordering:** bullet: chart at wave `0` creates the credentials namespace, `ExternalSecret` at wave `1`, fail-closed parenthetical kept (spec.4.fix-G3.1).
- **§25 retire row.** SPEC-12 (b) becomes the citation "Pool credential rotation follows [§4.9](...) Credential rotation workflow." (r2:spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **SPEC-14.** Adds the credentials namespace and its Secrets to the §18.38 Phase 17a uninstall residual list as a name plus a pointer to **Credentials namespace.** rule 1 (r2:spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **Cross-pool copy edge case.** Edge cases bullet 2's 400 covers a same-pool copy onto another id or a `platform-admin` cross-pool copy; a tenant-admin cross-pool copy gets 403 (r2:spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **Testing item 6.** Uses a `platform-admin` caller for the new-id prefixed-ref zero-probe case (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1).
- **0110 overlap.** Recorded only in summary `## Impacts on other proposals`; if 0112 lands first, 0110 inserts **Session ownership.** after the ¹ footnote (f1.other-proposals-0110).
- **Open decisions.** Summary reads "No decision is open." (f1.human-decisions).
- **Tenant-viewer code defect.** Credential-pool GETs are gated on `manage_credential_pools`, which `RoleTenantViewer` lacks; §10.2 wins; filed in summary defects (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **Re-enable gate.** Code gates re-enable on `credPoolAdmin`, so SPEC-7 (d) matches code and is not a control regression (spec.4.review-security.1).
- **CLI flags.** `add-credential` takes `--id`, `--secret-ref`, `--role-arn`, `--region`; a roleArn add introduces no secretRef (spec.1.review-client-surface.1).
- **MCP path.** lenny-ops forwards the caller's own bearer, so the platform-admin rule is not bypassed via `lenny_credential_add` (spec.1.review-client-surface.1).
- **Bootstrap gate.** `POST /v1/admin/bootstrap` requires platform-admin, so the seed exception gives no tenant-admin bypass (r2:spec.1.review-security.1).
- **Erasure actor.** Tenant deletion Phase 4 runs in the gateway process (spec.4.review-reliability.1).
- **Row lock cost.** Only admin handlers and bootstrap take the `credential_pools` row lock; the lease hot path does not (spec.4.review-performance.1).
- **Rule 7 holds.** No chart ClusterRole grants a verb on `secrets`; only release-namespace Roles do (spec.4.review-security.1).
- **Gateway-only identity.** The Token Service accepts only gateway certificates (spec.4.review-security.1).
- **deletecollection RBAC.** Authorized on the verb alone, no `list`; it cannot be label-restricted (spec.4.fix-G1.1, spec.4.fix-design-G1.1).
- **Locators.** Every SPEC-1..SPEC-14 locator resolved once after redesign 1 (r2:spec.1.review-applicability.2, r2:spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **No gate pins removed text.** Only `pkg/preflight/install_prereqs_test.go` pins the etcdctl string (CODE-7) (spec.1.review-applicability.1, r2:spec.1.review-applicability.2).
- **Plan scope.** 0112 claims no remediation-plan step; plan-conformance has empty scope (r2:spec.1.review-plan-conformance.1).
- **Uncached per-read rule.** Not filed as a performance finding; owner-mandated "no stale value" (r2:spec.1.review-performance.1).
- **Out of scope Secrets.** `clientSecretRef: lenny-system/github-client-secret`, `lenny-admin-token`, `postgres-credentials` are not pool Secrets (r2:spec.1.review-edit-sites.1).
- **§4.6.3 non-exhaustive.** Omitting the credential-writer Role there is not a missed site (spec.4.review-edit-sites.1).
- **Region-only update.** Full-entry replacement in code drops a Lenny-written ref, and the catch-all row deletes it, consistent with the table (r2:spec.1.review-mechanism.1).
- **Audit events.** Pool credential writes already audit `admin.credential_pool.credential_*` (r2:spec.1.review-security.1).
- **Logging list.** Rule 7's logging-exclusion list mirrors a pre-existing pattern; Role YAML is illustration (spec.4.review-single-source.1, r2:spec.1.review-single-source.1).

### Traps

- **Lease cache wording.** Do not write "caches no Secret value" for every Token Service read; the in-memory lease credential cache (§4.9 LLM Reverse Proxy, §13.3 proxy mode) holds materialized material. Rule 2 forbids only a Secret cache serving a read (spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **SPEC-7 row wording.** Keep "subject to the rules and outcomes named on pool creation"; do not rewrite as "an introduced `secretRef` is checked", because rules 1 and 3 apply to every submitted ref (spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **CODE-3 step order.** Step 5 must run after step 4 (else tenant-admin gets 412 instead of 403) and after step 6 (else an introduced `lenny-cred-` ref gets 412 on every retry instead of 400) (spec.1.fix-G2.1, spec.1.fix-design-G2.1).
- **Introduced set before mutation.** CODE-3 step 4 must compute the introduced set from submitted refs before the closure applies the mutation; computed after, it is empty and the 403 fails open (spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **No 400-before-probe promise.** Do not reinstate a request-level "400 before any probe". A platform-admin request mixing a probe-eligible ref with a refused `lenny-cred-` ref legitimately returns 422 or 503. The withdrawn option was request-level "400 or 403" wording; the per-reference sentence replaced it (spec.2.fix-G1.1, spec.2.fix-design-G1.1, spec.2.follow-up-fix.1).
- **Zero-probe tests.** Any zero-probe test pinning the IsManaged exclusion, or the cross-pool reserved-prefix 400, must use a `platform-admin`; a tenant-admin gets 403 first (spec.1.follow-up-fix.1, r2:spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **Runbook step (5).** Name only the endpoint; no role or source list. Step (6) keeps "external secrets manager" (spec.1.fix-G4.1).
- **NET-040 list.** Do not re-list components in the callout, and do not fix the §13.2.4 webhook/controller rows inside SPEC-6 (filed as summary defect **Webhook and controller kube-apiserver egress rows.**). Webhooks do make apiserver reads despite "purely in-process validators" (spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.1.fix-design-G5.1).
- **Compensating delete withdrawn.** Do not reinstate a compensating Secret delete under a new row lock after a failed commit, nor its "no entry carries its ref" guard or "compensating delete also fails" case. The crash window leaves the same orphan, so label cleanup and the rule 5 orphan row are needed anyway. "A failed commit after Put leaves an orphan" is the accepted residue row; test 8 asserts no Delete. Reason lives once in summary **Decisions** (redesign.1).
- **Enumerated removal list withdrawn.** Do not reinstate old rule 4's path list (credential removal, pool update, forced seed, ref-replacing update, pool deletion). The rule 3 catch-all row "Delete of each dropped reference" replaced it; the earlier list omitted the bootstrap force-update (redesign.1, spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **Dropped reference snapshot.** "Dropped reference" = Lenny-written ref on the locked row before the write and not after; CODE-4 step 4 snapshots before `applyCredentialPoolUpdate` or the seed closure overwrites `p.Credentials`. The seed closure replaces `pool.Credentials` wholesale even with a nil list, and clears `DeletedAt` (revives a soft-deleted pool) (redesign.1, spec.1.fix-design-G3.1).
- **Delete before commit.** Rule 4 keeps Delete before commit, so the "reference to an absent Secret" residue row stands; not a contradiction (redesign.1).
- **Rule 6 trigger.** Word it as "at least one Put or Delete" (CODE-4 "non-empty"), not "a write that rule 3 gives a Put or a Delete", which refuses every pool write. Widened rule 6 closes no-writer removal paths. `dryRun` is refused like the write, including the seed `opts.dryRun` path (redesign.1).
- **No-writer erasure.** Phase 4 fails only while some pool (soft-deleted included) carries a Lenny-written ref; the check must read rows before `DeleteByTenant`, or it is vacuous. The unnamed orphan then is a stated outcome (redesign.1, r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Erasure order.** Delete-collection runs after `DeleteByTenant`; ordered first, a concurrent platform-admin `secretData` Put leaves an unreclaimed Secret. Test 10 must assert `DeleteTenant` runs after `DeleteByTenant` returns; a post-state check passes either way. Withdrawn: running it both before and after (r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1, r2:spec.1.review-reliability.1).
- **Label on both branches.** `Writer.Put` must set the tenant-hash label on Create and on the AlreadyExists→Update branch; Update replaces the object (spec.4.fix-G1.1).
- **Never-deletes sentence.** Do not reinstate it; the delete-collection removes any Secret carrying the label, operator-created ones included (r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Caller-tenant clause.** Keep it on rule 4's keyed read; the Token Service reads namespace-wide and cannot enforce it (r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1).
- **Code mechanics.** Do not hand-set `Retry-After` (`writeError` does it); use `!p.IsActive()` since `DeletedAt` is `time.Time`; `writeCredentialMutateError` maps unknown errors to 400, so the writer sentinels and post-writer commit failure need explicit 503; CODE-5 reads the writer from a `gatewayWiring` field, not threaded signatures (redesign.1).
- **Summary phrases.** "Delete-before-write under the row lock" is gone; the source-switch non-goal points at rule 3 (redesign.1).
- **No "before the Token Service starts".** Do not reintroduce "must populate the Secrets before the Token Service starts resolving them"; per-read fail-closed allows later Secrets (spec.4.fix-G3.1).
- **GitHub provider row.** The §4.9 `github` Credential Provider and lease-TTL rows describe minted App tokens, disagreeing with the stored `token` read. Filed as summary defect **GitHub provider source material.**; needs a mint-versus-store design round (spec.4.fix-G2.1).
- **Rotation branches.** Keep any by-reference/by-value branch list out of the §25 retire row and the DOCS-1 agent-operability row; docs/operator-guide/agent-operability.md:253 "revoke + add" must become a reader-facing pointer (r2:spec.1.fix-G2.1, r2:spec.1.fix-design-G2.1).
- **Partial design index.** The spec-changes **Admin-time Secret probe (required).** design paragraph omits SPEC-4, 13, 14; do not grow it. The summary index is complete (r2:spec.1.fix-G3.1).
- **Keep bullet 2 second sentence.** "A platform-admin cannot point one tenant's pool at another tenant's Lenny-written Secret" stays true (r2:spec.1.fix-design-G4.1).
- **412 on POST.** Already DEFERRED to the non-spec loop; do not refile against the spec (r2:spec.1.review-mechanism.1).
- **Seed is not public PUT.** Force-update runs in bootstrap code; do not file a probe bypass without reading bootstrap_resources.go (spec.2.review-fresh.1).
- **Glob trap.** `*spec-changes.md` matches spec and non-spec files; name the file explicitly (spec.4.fix-G3.1).
- **Lookup padding.** lookup exact missed the "Egress Allowed" header due to table padding; grep it (spec.2.review-fresh.1).
- **Metric collision.** `lenny_token_service_secret_reloads_total` already counts KEK reloads in promemit; SPEC-8 redefines it. Non-spec loop must reconcile (spec.4.review-performance.1).
- **Materialization perf.** SPEC-3 rule 2 would put dormant LLM lease materialization on a synchronous apiserver GET (~200/s at Tier 3) and make lease renewal apiserver-dependent, against §4.6.1. Not filed; revisit in the materialization proposal (spec.1.review-performance.1).
- **Single-source note.** SPEC-2 (g) rule 4 repeated summary bootstrap-no-probe reasoning near verbatim; keep the reason in the summary (spec.1.review-single-source.1). Not confirmed closed.
- **Reinstall consequence.** The keep annotation blocking a reinstall under a different release name is in DOCS-1 only, never in spec text or Edge cases (spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1, r2:spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1).
- **Heading name.** §10.2 is "Authentication"; the matrix is under "#### Authorization and RBAC"; the SPEC-5 title is not a defect (r2:spec.1.review-citations.1).
- **Plan lens.** Empty findings are correct unless a round adds a plan-step claim (r2:spec.1.review-plan-conformance.1).

### Open

- **Revocation runbook kubectl.** docs/runbooks/credential-revocation.md Step 4 still uses `kubectl -n lenny-system`; DOCS-1 omits it (spec.1.fix-G4.1, spec.1.fix-design-G4.1).
- **Gateway-only CIDR wording.** NET-040 lead-in (spec/13) and §17.6 kube-apiserver CIDR preflight row and values comment still say gateway egress; unstaged, unfiled (spec.1.fix-G5.1, spec.4.review-edit-sites.1).

- **installation.md Step 3.** Creates the LLM Secret in lenny-system before the chart; DOCS-1 does not list installation.md (spec.4.fix-G3.1, spec.4.fix-design-G3.1).
- **Writes to a deleting tenant.** Nothing refuses platform-admin pool writes against a `deleting` tenant; pre-existing across Phase 4 stores; decide whether summary defects records it (r2:spec.1.fix-G1.1, r2:spec.1.fix-design-G1.1).
- **lenny-ctl.md rotation block.** docs/operator-guide/lenny-ctl.md "Credential Rotation" shows create-new-Secret plus add plus remove; check DOCS-1 coverage (r2:spec.1.fix-G2.1).
- **tenant_id spelling.** §15.1 create row says `?tenant_id=`; handlers read `tenantId`; SPEC-7 (a) carries it forward (r2:spec.1.review-client-surface.1).
- **Data classification row.** spec/12 "Credential pool secrets | T4 | Postgres" disagrees with §4.9; pre-existing; candidate summary defect (r2:spec.1.review-edit-sites.1).
- UNVERIFIED: **roleArn by tenant-admin.** A tenant-admin can name any IAM role ARN for the Token Service to assume; pre-existing; owner or security pass decides (r2:spec.1.review-security.1).

Today `rest.InClusterConfig()` with client-go defaults (5 QPS, burst 10) gives about 20 GET/s across 4 Tier 3 replicas, while CODE-6 puts about 2 keyed reads on each authenticated gitClone session (up to 400 GET/s at 200 sessions/s). Throttled reads surface as TRANSIENT session-creation failures. Code-only remedy. (spec.4.review-performance.1, r2:spec.1.review-performance.1)
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-3 step 3 computes the probed set with newSecretRefs against the UNLOCKED read, while SPEC-2 **Registering a Secret by reference.** decides the introduced set at the locked row and the probe paragraph probes "each introduced reference". A platform-admin resubmission of a ref a concurrent write removed is introduced at the lock but never probed. Remedy is code-side (re-check or probe the locked-row delta), owned by the non-spec loop. (spec.2.review-fresh.1)
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-3 step 5 maps an unprobed locked-row introduced ref to `412 ETAG_MISMATCH` on every path, including `POST .../credentials`, which takes no If-Match; §15.1 defines ETAG_MISMATCH as an If-Match mismatch, and no staged spec text names a 412 for this case. A code-only remedy exists (handler re-probes the uncovered delta and retries the Update); the non-spec loop picks the code remedy or has the spec name the outcome. (spec.4.review-client-surface.1)
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: OpenAPI re-enable route role must follow SPEC-7 (d) (manage_credential_pools / tenant-admin) and the credential POST/PUT request schemas must gain `secretData`; owned by the CODE/DOCS loop. (r2:spec.1.review-client-surface.1)
- DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: if a fixer adds the no-Token-Service VCS read outcome to SPEC-3 (c), DOCS-1 should carry it to the operator page that describes the dev profile (page not identified). Context: CODE-6 step 2 removes the gateway's own `connectorsecret.NewKubeResolver`, so with no Token Service a VCS read has no resolver, and no staged spec text says so. (spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1)

### [spec.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 (f) values rule 5's table is indexed by Secret state (orphan; entry references an absent Lenny-written Secret; entry's Secret holds a value other than the last committed submission), with a lead bullet saying the row lock does not order an operation the kube-apiserver applies after the lock's transaction ended — BECAUSE the old cause-indexed table ("failed op, failed commit, crash between") broke on the first unlisted cause (late-landing Put/Delete after crash or Postgres failover); three states partition every disagreement between a deterministic name and the rows — ALTERNATIVES: a fifth late-landing row (same enumeration, one cause longer); per-op deadline plus repeated Phase 4 delete-collection (a client deadline does not bound when the apiserver applies an accepted request); resourceVersion preconditions (cannot fence a Create); ownerReference to a per-tenant owner (new object, RBAC, UID tracking); refuse writes to a deleting tenant (straddles the transition; already Open); per-write nonce names (reverses deterministic naming).
WATCHOUT: Do not reinstate a cause list in rule 5, a "serialize" claim for concurrent pool writes, or any erasure-completeness claim for Lenny-written Secrets. An orphan created after the Phase 4 delete-collection (late create after a crash or failover) is an accepted outcome; the reason lives once in the summary Decisions bullet on the pool row lock. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credentialpoolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go Store.Update (SELECT ... FOR UPDATE inside pgtenant.InTx, mutate runs inside); spec/12_storage-architecture.md "RTO: < 30s (automatic failover)".
WATCHOUT: Do not add a deadline, delay, or second delete-collection to Phase 4, SPEC-9, CODE-5, or test 10; do not add a late-landing test. Each state row is reachable with test 8's existing commit-failure injection (Put on add then commit fails: orphan; Delete then commit fails: absent; in-place Put then commit fails: stale value).
MISTAKE: Run 2 round 2 recorded that the row lock makes DeleteByTenant wait for an admin Put so the later delete-collection removes the Secret; that holds only while the writer's transaction is live.
CORRECTS [spec.2.review-reliability.1]: its FACT "An admin Put racing tenant deletion runs under the pool row lock (rule 4), so DeleteByTenant waits for it and the later delete-collection removes the Secret" is false when the gateway crashes or Postgres fails over with the Put in flight: the lock is released and the apiserver can apply the create after the delete-collection. Compaction should drop it.
CORRECTS [spec.2.review-fresh.1]: its FACT "no new Lenny-written Secret can appear after the delete-collection runs" is false for the same late-landing reason; it holds only for operations issued after DeleteByTenant.
FACT: The related-passages lookup over spec, docs, and the proposal returned zero dependents for both changed statements; the manual grep (residue, interrupted, in-place overwrite, serializ, orphan, rule 5, tenant deletion deletes/removes, erasure deletes) found only the sites edited plus still-true hits (summary 12 and 70, non-spec tests 8 and 10, CODE-5 step 1, spec-changes Edge cases bullet on accepted residues).

### [spec.3.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 (d) **Registering a Secret by reference.** now ends with the check order: **Secret references.** rule 1 (400), then the registration check (403), then rule 3 (400), and a write receives the code of the first check that refuses it. BECAUSE rule 1 must run first (the introduced set compares canonical forms, and CODE-3 step 2 parses before any store call), and 403 must precede rule 3 (a tenant-admin cross-pool `lenny-cred-` copy gets 403, r2:spec.1.fix-G4.1). ALTERNATIVES: 403 before every 400 (no canonical form exists for a rule-1-refused ref, and the order would need a pre-parse role check); order stated in **Secret references.** opening (the seed runs rules 1 and 3 and no registration check); redefining rule 1's refused value as "not a reference" (leaves 403-versus-rule-3 unstated); restating in the Edge cases bullet (second statement).
CORRECTS [spec.3.review-mechanism.1]: the Settled line "Mixed-request precedence. The spec states no precedence among simultaneous refusals" is now false for rule 1, the registration check, and rule 3, whose order the registration paragraph states. It still holds for the probe relative to rule 3 (Traps, "No 400-before-probe promise").
WATCHOUT: the precedence sentence orders only rule 1, the registration check, and rule 3. Do not extend it to the probe, and do not restate it in the Edge cases bullet, the summary, or the §15.1 rows. CODE-3 step 6 carries a pointer ("It runs after step 4"), and Testing item 5 carries the one discriminating case (tenant-admin: 403 for an uncarried `lenny-cred-` ref, 400 for a foreign namespace). Test 6 keeps its platform-admin caller. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 (d); non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 6, Testing item 5.

### [spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Rule 5's residue table is re-indexed by Secret state relative to the committed rows (orphan; entry references an absent Secret; entry's Secret holds a value other than the last value a committed write submitted), replacing the "Interrupted operation" table and the cause list "A failed Secret operation, a row change that fails to commit ..., and a gateway crash between the two". The three states partition every disagreement between a deterministic Lenny-written name and the rows, so the table is complete whatever the cause. The summary's "concurrent writes to one pool serialize their Secret operations" clause is deleted, not qualified. — BECAUSE the cause-indexed list failed the moment a new cause (an apiserver applying a request after the Postgres transaction ended on a gateway crash or a Postgres failover) was named; adding that cause as a row is the next enumeration and fails the same way. — ALTERNATIVES: (a) the finding's extra residue row (same kind of answer as before, one cause longer); (b) a client deadline on each Secret operation plus a repeated Phase 4 delete-collection after it (a client timeout does not bound when the kube-apiserver or etcd applies a request it has already received, so it would put a false completeness claim in the spec); (c) resourceVersion preconditions stored on the row (needs per-Secret state in Postgres, and a precondition cannot fence a Create, so the post-erasure orphan stays); (d) ownerReferences to a per-tenant owner object so Kubernetes GC collects late creates (closes the erasure case, but adds an owner object, configmap RBAC, and UID tracking for a writer with no `get`); (e) refusing pool writes to a `deleting` tenant (narrows the window only; already an Open item).
DECISION: An orphan created after the tenant-deletion delete-collection is an accepted residue, recorded once in the summary Decisions bullet on the row lock. It is consistent with rule 6's accepted no-writer orphan and with the no-sweeper decision. — BECAUSE no fence closes it without a new mechanism (see (b) to (d) above).
WATCHOUT: The pool row lock is `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` inside `pgtenant.InTx`, and the Writer calls run inside `mutate`. A gateway crash or a Postgres failover ends the transaction and releases the lock while a Secret request may still be applied by the kube-apiserver. Do not write any sentence saying the lock orders or serializes Secret operations across writers, or that tenant deletion removes every Lenny-written Secret. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credentialpoolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:133-148; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:151 (RTO < 30s)
FACT: §12.8 Phase 3 commits `TenantState` `deleting` and the gateway then rejects the tenant's own bearer tokens, but nothing refuses platform-admin pool writes, so a write can start during Phase 3.5 or 4. `DeleteByTenant` on `credential_pools` blocks on a held `FOR UPDATE` lock, and the delete-collection runs directly after it. — EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:924,926; pgstore.go:271-283
CORRECTS [spec.2.review-reliability.1]: "An admin Put racing tenant deletion runs under the pool row lock (rule 4), so DeleteByTenant waits for it and the later delete-collection removes the Secret" holds only while the writer's transaction is live. A Put still in flight when a crash or a failover ends that transaction can be applied after the delete-collection. Compaction should drop that FACT.
DECISION: No late-landing test case is added. Every state the new table names can be reached with the existing commit-failure and Delete-failure injection (test 8), and asserting that a delayed operation lands in "some rule 5 row" is a tautology over a partition.

### [spec.3.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: Single-reference precedence is rule 1 (400) -> **Registering a Secret by reference.** (403) -> rule 3 (400), stated once as one sentence appended to the SPEC-2 (d) **Registering a Secret by reference.** paragraph; CODE-3 step 6 gains a pointer "runs after step 4"; test 5 gains one tenant-admin case pinning both edges — BECAUSE rule 1 must run first (the introduced set compares canonical forms, and a foreign-namespace or malformed ref has no canonical form; CODE-3 step 2 already parses before any store call), and 403-before-rule-3 is already settled (r2:spec.1.fix-G4.1 tenant-admin cross-pool copy gets 403; the zero-probe trap relies on it) — ALTERNATIVES: 403 first for everything (authz-before-validation) rejected because the introduced-set check cannot be computed on an unparseable ref and it would reverse the settled Edge case; putting the order in **Secret references.** opening rejected because the bootstrap seed applies rules 1 and 3 with no registration check, so the order belongs with the check it is relative to.
CORRECTS [spec.3.review-mechanism.1 / Settled "Mixed-request precedence"]: after this fix the spec does state precedence among rule 1, the registration check, and rule 3 (also for a mixed request: any rule-1-refused ref gives 400; otherwise any introduced ref from a non-platform-admin gives 403 before rule 3). It still states no precedence between the probe outcomes (422/503) and rule 3, and the "No 400-before-probe promise" trap stands.
WATCHOUT: Do not word the new sentence as "400 before any probe" or "400 or 403"; it orders the three checks only. The probe's place stays as SPEC-2 (g) opening states it. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 (g) opening (line ~167); Traps "No 400-before-probe promise".

### [spec.3.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no spec-scope performance or failure-mode finding in round 3 — BECAUSE the round's changes (delete-collection moved after `DeleteByTenant`, rule 6 "Before the CredentialPoolStore deletion" scoping, SPEC-14 residual list, SPEC-12 (b) citation) add no write path and no store dependency; a Phase 4 retry after `DeleteByTenant` succeeded and delete-collection failed re-runs an idempotent `DeleteByTenant` and a label-scoped delete-collection, so a crash or gateway handoff mid-phase converges; a concurrent secretData write either holds the row lock (DeleteByTenant waits, then delete-collection removes the Secret) or starts after and gets 404 before any Put (rule 4) — ALTERNATIVES: refiling the uncached per-read GET rate (settled, owner-mandated; archive spec.1.review-performance.1 already quantified about 400 GET/s at Tier 3 against client-go defaults and deferred the QPS fix to CODE-2).

### [spec.3.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The pool row lock is a Postgres `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` inside `pgtenant.InTx`; the Secret Put/Delete runs inside the mutate closure, so the lock ends when the connection drops (gateway crash, Postgres failover) while the kube-apiserver request may still be applied afterwards. The lock serializes Postgres writers but does not fence a late-landing apiserver write. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credentialpoolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:129-148; spec-changes.md:144,150
DECISION: filed the unfenced late Secret write (stale overwrite after a later 200, Delete landing after a re-add, create landing after the tenant delete-collection) as one design-defect — BECAUSE rule 5 claims its residue table is complete for crash and commit failure — ALTERNATIVES: a per-operation timeout alone was rejected as the remedy because a client cancel does not stop an apiserver write already accepted.
WATCHOUT: Do not re-file the rule 6 Phase 4 retry after the rows are gone with the Token Service disabled; spec.2.review-reliability.1 already settled it as accepted residue. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:159
USEFUL [spec.2.review-reliability.1]: confirmed DeleteByTenant-then-delete-collection is crash-safe on retry, so this round only had to look at in-flight apiserver writes.

DECISION: The rule 5 residue table's orphan row Convergence cell now ends "An orphan created after that delete-collection stays in the credentials namespace." It is landing text for the outcome the summary Decisions bullet on the pool row lock accepts. A Phase 4 re-run was rejected because it adds a mechanism the WATCHOUT forbids.

Same precedent as the round-3 deletion of the bullet that restated SPEC-3 (c) rule 3 — ALTERNATIVES: cutting it to a pointer (the section header already says each case is owned by named staged text, and a pointer bullet for a case the home covers adds no case).

### [spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 (f) rule 6 refuses with a new PERMANENT `422 TOKEN_SERVICE_NOT_CONFIGURED` row, added in SPEC-7 (i) right after the `CREDENTIAL_PROBE_UNAVAILABLE` row. SPEC-7 (j) cites rule 5 only, and the Design sentence drops "no error code". BECAUSE no deployment-level answer is TRANSIENT (§15.1 `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` covers "a dependency it consulted returned a transient error"), and no existing catalog code's definition covers "this deployment has no Token Service". `KMS_REGION_UNRESOLVABLE` (PERMANENT 422, "no KMS key is configured") is the catalog precedent, and `EXPORT_NOT_CONFIGURED` (cmd/lenny-gateway/mcpsurface.go) is the naming precedent. ALTERNATIVES: `VALIDATION_ERROR` 400 does not fit pool DELETE, which has no body. `CONFIGURATION_CONFLICT` and `INVALID_POOL_CONFIGURATION` are about request fields. `INVALID_STATE_TRANSITION` is about resource state. Two codes (400 for secretData and another for Deletes) would add a split to fix one defect.
DECISION: the seed's nil-writer per-entry code stays `SEED_STORE_ERROR`. BECAUSE rule 6 names no seed code, and §17.6 exit 2 ("some resources failed, others succeeded") stays true. ALTERNATIVES: `SEED_VALIDATION` (exit 1) would match the permanence, but it is an unreviewed cascade, and its comment says "before any store mutation", which a check inside the Update closure does not clearly satisfy.
DECISION: rule 1 now reads "Pool creation and pool update refuse `secretData` with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR`, and the bootstrap seed reports it as an error on that pool entry." BECAUSE every other seed refusal is a per-entry error, as Secret references rule 2, rules 5 and 6, and CODE-3 step 7 state.
FACT: an unknown error code at a 4xx classifies as PERMANENT, not retryable, through `errorclassify.ClassifyStatus`. Classify (no status) falls back to TRANSIENT, retryable, so a new code still needs an explicit `table` entry. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/errorclassify/errorclassify.go:44-76
FACT: no gate checks parity between docs/reference/error-catalog.md and the §15.1 catalog. That page already omits `CREDENTIAL_PROBE_UNAVAILABLE`, so a new catalog row puts no docs page under a gate. EVIDENCE: grep of tests/ and scripts/ for "error-catalog"; docs/reference/error-catalog.md:102

### [spec.7.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 **Secret references.** rule 1 defines the accepted form (`<name>` or `<credentials namespace>/<name>`, `<name>` a DNS-1123 subdomain name) and refuses "Any other reference" with 400 VALIDATION_ERROR, details.field secretRef — BECAUSE an enumerated refusal list is a second statement of the predicate and omitted the invalid-name case (and gave the bare form no name constraint) — ALTERNATIVES: appending a fourth refusal conjunct (rejected: lengthens the drifting list).
WATCHOUT: Do not reinstate an enumerated refusal list in rule 1 or in CODE-1 prose; CODE-1 now points at rule 1 ("accepts the forms SPEC-2 **Secret references.** rule 1 defines and refuses every other reference"). TEST-1 test 1's input table (bare, qualified, foreign ns, three segments, empty segments, invalid DNS-1123 name, reserved prefix) is a case list, not a restatement, and stays. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 (d) rule 1; non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 prose.

### [spec.7.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 (d) **Secret references.** rule 1 refuses the complement of its own definition ("Any other reference is refused ...") instead of enumerating refusal conditions; CODE-1's prose cuts its enumeration to a pointer at rule 1 — BECAUSE an enumerated refusal list is a second statement of the accepted form and drifts from it (the invalid-name case went missing); defining the accepted form once and refusing everything else cannot omit a case — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's suggestion (append a fourth conjunct "or whose name is not a DNS-1123 subdomain name") is the same enumeration one item longer and leaves the next omitted case as the next finding.

### [spec.7.review-performance.1]
- FACT: The gateway kube client runs at QPS 100 and burst 200 (`--cluster-qps`/`--cluster-burst`), and neither the spec nor the code sets a Postgres idle_in_transaction timeout. A pool deletion that issues N Secret Deletes under one row lock therefore holds the transaction for about N/100 s at most. That is not a top-tier bottleneck, because these are admin-path writes only. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:875-878, cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:171-172
- DECISION: returned no findings for round 7 under the performance lens. BECAUSE every write the staged spec adds is on the admin path (credential add, update, remove, and pool delete) or is a single per-tenant deletecollection, and none touches the session or lease hot path. The Postgres-failover residue is owned by rule 5, and Redis is not involved. ALTERNATIVES: refiling the uncached per-read rule or the materialization latency, both of which are already settled in Standing context as owner-mandated or deferred.
- USEFUL [spec.4.review-performance.1]: the row-lock cost note (the lease hot path takes no `credential_pools` row lock) settled the contention question.

### [spec.7.review-reliability.1]
- FACT: §12.8 **Idempotency and resumption.** says tenant deletion resumes from the last incomplete phase and each phase is individually idempotent, so SPEC-2 (f) rule 5 "the job stays at Phase 4 and retries the phase" re-runs earlier `DeleteByTenant` steps harmlessly and the label delete-collection is itself idempotent. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:946
- FACT: A partial failure of a multi-Delete write (pool update or pool deletion) without any crash still lands in residue row 2. Rule 3 deletes only dropped refs, and rule 2 names are deterministic per (tenant, pool, credId), so a write never mixes a Put with a Delete of a still-referenced Secret. The partial state therefore affects only Secrets the write was dropping anyway. Not a finding. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:132-153
- FACT: DeleteByTenant on CredentialPoolStore waits on the admin write's row lock, so an in-flight secretData add either commits before the deletion (and the later delete-collection reclaims its Secret) or sees no row (404, no Put). Only a late-applied apiserver op after a crash yields a post-collection orphan, which the orphan row states. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:142,152
- WATCHOUT: With no Token Service, rule 6 makes Phase 4 retry indefinitely while any pool carries a Lenny-written ref. Nothing but re-enabling the Token Service clears that state, because every ref-dropping write is refused. This is the stated fail-closed design. Do not file it as a wedge without new evidence. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:156
- FACT: The replaced live-probe paragraph named no probe timeout value, so SPEC-2 (g) drops no deadline. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1697

### [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30e]

- DECISION (orchestrator under the owner's standing delegation, 2026-10-30): open decision 1 takes the staged chart guards and the documented operator obligation, plus a preflight check that warns on every Secret in the credentials namespace that no credential pool references and Lenny did not write. It warns rather than fails, because variant A creates a Secret before it is registered.

### [spec.9.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: deleted the three preflight-check sentences from summary `## Open decisions for human to make` (decision 1). Decision 1 now rests on the staged chart guards (render guard, `pods: "0"` quota, keep annotation) plus the documented operator obligation — BECAUSE listing Secrets needs `list` on `secrets`, a Secret list returns `.data`, and RBAC cannot restrict it to metadata, so the check would give `lenny-preflight` read access to every tenant's credential values, which breaks SPEC-2 (f) **Credentials namespace.** rule 7 and rule 3's deliberate withholding of `list` — ALTERNATIVES: stage a §17.6 Checks performed row and amend rule 7 (breaks the fail-closed predicate; the pre-install hook also runs before the namespace and any pool row exist); an operator-run `lenny-ctl` check (§17.6 **CLI equivalent:** runs the same checks as the Job, so a CLI-only check splits the two paths, and it needs new SPEC, CODE, TEST, and DOCS deliverables).
CORRECTS [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30e]: decision 1 no longer includes the preflight warning on unreferenced Secrets in the credentials namespace. The staged spec never carried it, and it cannot run under rule 7. Compaction should reword the review-log line that records the decision.
WATCHOUT: do not reintroduce a preflight or any other Lenny-run listing of Secrets in the credentials namespace. Any `list` grant there exposes credential values to a third identity. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/preflight-job.yaml (the lenny-preflight ClusterRole and Role carry no `secrets` rule); spec-changes.md SPEC-2 (f) rule 7.
OPEN: should an operator-run, read-only `lenny-ctl` listing of unreferenced Secrets in the credentials namespace exist under the operator's own credentials? This is for the orchestrator. It is not staged, and the proposal is complete without it.
FACT: status.md still describes decision 1 as an open preflight-check decision. That was already stale since ec3a28467 and is a separate finding (not edited here).

### [spec.9.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: Delete the three preflight-check sentences from summary `## Open decisions for human to make` (decision 1). Decision 1 then rests on the chart guards and the documented operator obligation, and **Credentials namespace.** rule 7 and the Design bullet stay unchanged — BECAUSE the check needs `list` on `secrets` in the credentials namespace for the `lenny-preflight` ServiceAccount. A Secret `list` returns `.data`, and RBAC cannot limit it to metadata, so the grant would give a third identity every tenant's credential values. That contradicts rule 7, rule 3 (no `list` even for the Token Service), and the summary Goal "Only the Token Service reads ... in the credentials namespace". Problem-statement item 1 accepts the chart as the guard, so the item is met without the check — ALTERNATIVES: (a) Stage a §17.6 Checks performed row plus a `list` grant for `lenny-preflight`. Rejected because it breaks rule 7 and the Goal. As a pre-install hook it would also run before the namespace and any pool row exist. (b) An operator-run `lenny-ctl` check under the operator's own kubeconfig. Rejected because §24.2 / §17.6 **CLI equivalent:** says the CLI "runs the same checks" as the Job, so a CLI-only check splits the two paths. It would also need SPEC-10, SPEC-11, CODE, TEST, and DOCS deliverables for a warning-only listing. (c) Extend summary Non-goals line "A preflight check over RoleBindings ..." with the listing. Rejected under the edit ladder, because the reason has its home here in the log.
CORRECTS [orchestrator.decisions-2026-10-30e]: the "plus a preflight check that warns on every Secret in the credentials namespace ..." half cannot be built under the staged RBAC (see the DECISION above). The rest of the decision stands as staged: chart guards (keep annotation, `pods: "0"` quota, and render guard) plus the documented operator obligation.

### [spec.9.review-reliability.1]

FACT: A Phase 4 action error is wrapped `errPhaseRetryable`, and the controller keeps the job at that phase for a later pass, so SPEC-2 (f) rule 5 "The job stays at Phase 4 and retries the phase" matches the tree. Each Phase 4 retry re-runs `DeleteByTenant` and then `DeleteTenant`, and both are idempotent. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/tenantdeletion/controller.go:234-240, :341, :486
FACT: `pgstore.Create` returns `ErrAlreadyExists` while a soft-deleted row holds the pool name, so a re-created pool cannot reach an old soft-deleted pool's deterministic `lenny-cred-` names. Only the seed revival path (see Traps) reaches them. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credentialpoolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:54-98
DECISION: did not file "a commit failure after a Secret operation is not given a code by rule 5" — BECAUSE the §15.1 `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` definition already covers a transient backing-store error (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1139), so CODE-4 step 5's 503 has a spec basis — ALTERNATIVES: adding a rule 5 clause was rejected as additive text with no correctness gain.
DECISION: did not file the no-writer Phase 4 wedge (soft-deleted pools keep Lenny-written refs after pool deletion, so a tenant whose deployment later drops the Token Service cannot finish Phase 4 until the Token Service is re-enabled) — BECAUSE rule 6 states this outcome as intended fail-closed behavior, and re-enabling the Token Service is the recovery path.

### [spec.11.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-2 (f) **Credential values submitted through the admin API.** rule 1 gains "A `secretData` with no keys, or whose data Kubernetes Secret validation refuses, is refused with `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` and `details.field: "secretData"`." CODE-4 step 1 names `validation.IsConfigMapKey` per key and `corev1.MaxSecretSize` on the total; test 7 reads "One case per rule 1 sentence" — BECAUSE without a decode check an invalid key reaches the apiserver as 422 Invalid and rule 5 maps it to retryable TRANSIENT 503 (spec/15 `SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` row vs `VALIDATION_ERROR` PERMANENT 400) — ALTERNATIVES: mapping apiserver Invalid to 400 in rule 5 (breaks rule 5 bullet 1 and makes dryRun return 200 where the write returns 400); spelling out the key regex, 253 cap, and 1 MiB in spec text (enumerates another system's validation, goes stale); per-provider key table (summary non-goal).
WATCHOUT: Do not move the secretData validity check into rule 5 or enumerate Kubernetes limits in the spec; the check is at decode by reference to Kubernetes Secret validation, so dryRun and the write agree — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-2 (f) rule 1 and rule 5 bullets 1 and 3.
FACT: `IsConfigMapKey` is at k8s.io/apimachinery@v0.31.4/pkg/util/validation/validation.go:462 and `MaxSecretSize = 1 * 1024 * 1024` at k8s.io/api@v0.31.4/core/v1/types.go:7237; both modules are in go.mod at v0.31.4 — EVIDENCE: go.mod:67,69.
FACT: The related lookup judged 242 candidates and found none at 0.7 for the rule 1 change. The hand grep found only CODE-4 step 1 and test 7 counting rule 1's content, and both are edited. The summary non-goal on per-provider keys (summary.md:87) and spec-changes overview "one error code" stay true.

### [spec.11.fix-design-G1.1]
- DECISION: an invalid `secretData` is refused at decode by one sentence appended to SPEC-2 (f) **Credential values submitted through the admin API.** rule 1: no keys, or data the Kubernetes Secret validation refuses, gives `400 VALIDATION_ERROR` with `details.field: "secretData"` — BECAUSE a decode check keeps rule 4 ("after every check of the request has passed") and rule 5 bullet 1 ("a 4xx performs no Secret operation") true and gives a `dryRun` the same answer as the real write — ALTERNATIVES: mapping an apiserver `Invalid` on the Put to 400 in rule 5 (rejected: it contradicts rule 5 bullet 1, and a `dryRun` performs no Put so it would answer 200 where the write answers 400); enumerating the key regex, the 253-character cap, and the 1 MiB total in the spec (rejected: that is a list of the apiserver's limits; the predicate is cited by reference to Kubernetes Secret validation, and CODE-4 step 1 names the symbols).
- FACT: the apiserver's Secret data check is `k8s.io/apimachinery/pkg/util/validation.IsConfigMapKey` per key plus `corev1.MaxSecretSize` (1 MiB) on the total, both in the vendored v0.31.4 modules already in go.mod. EVIDENCE: ~/go/pkg/mod/k8s.io/apimachinery@v0.31.4/pkg/util/validation/validation.go:461, ~/go/pkg/mod/k8s.io/api@v0.31.4/core/v1/types.go:7237
- WATCHOUT: the summary non-goal "Validating `secretData` keys against a per-provider table" stays true after this fix. The new rule-1 check is syntactic (Kubernetes key validity and size). It is not a per-provider table, so do not delete or reword that non-goal. EVIDENCE: summary.md non-goals
- WATCHOUT: an empty `secretData` object was ambiguous for the at-most-one check (`omitempty` only affects encoding, so `{}` decodes as a non-nil empty map). The "no keys" refusal settles it: a present `secretData` with no keys is a 400.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G3.1]

DECISION: CODE-3 step 4 is the one home for where steps 4, 6, and 3 run: the locked row inside each `credentialPools.Update` closure, the unlocked read in each `dryRun` branch, and no row in `handleCreateCredentialPool` (every submitted ref introduced; checks run ahead of its `dryRun` branch and `Create`). The probe moved into the closure on the same introduced set as the 403 — BECAUSE create has no closure, so a closure-only step 4 failed open for a tenant-admin pool create, and a probe set from an unlocked read is what forced the uncovered-ref 412 — ALTERNATIVES: a create-only sentence beside a closure-only step 4 (two homes, keeps the 412); wrapping create in Create-then-Update (extra store call and window); re-probe and retry Update once on an uncovered ref (loop with undefined last response).
FACT: `pgstore.Update` runs the mutate closure once inside one transaction and returns any closure error before the `UPDATE`, so a 422 or 503 from an in-closure probe persists nothing. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credentialpoolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go `(*Store).Update`.
FACT: today `handleCreateCredentialPool` probes at handler level and calls `credentialPools.Create` with no closure; `handleAddCredential` and `handleUpdateCredentialEntry` have no `dryRun` branch; only pool create and pool PUT do. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go `handleCreateCredentialPool`, `handleUpdateCredentialPool`, `handleAddCredential`, `handleUpdateCredentialEntry`.

If G4 did not land that, step 4's list names branches nothing builds. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 step 4, CODE-4 step 5.

DECISION: CODE-3 step 7 and Testing item 6 point at step 6 for the reserved-prefix predicate ("a reserved-prefix ref that step 6 refuses"; "a prefixed ref the existing entry does not carry") — BECAUSE step 6 is the one home on every path, bootstrap included — ALTERNATIVES: restating the same-id predicate in step 7 (second statement).

### [non-spec.1.fix-G4.1]
FACT: Pool create, pool PUT, and the bootstrap body decode leniently into `CredentialPoolPayload`, whose entries have no `secretData`, so the field vanished before rule 1 could see it. Add and update are the only handlers with no `dryRun` branch today. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go:457-458 (create), :653-654 (PUT), :505-514 and :707-719 (the only dryRun branches), :798-923 (add and update, no dryRun); bootstrap.go:69 (`BootstrapRequest.CredentialPools []CredentialPoolPayload`), :353 (`upsertCredentialPools` takes `[]CredentialPoolPayload`).
DECISION: CODE-4 step 1 now routes pool create, pool PUT, and the bootstrap seed through a request-only pool type whose entries are `credentialEntryRequest` — BECAUSE it reuses the step 1 request type and keeps `CredentialEntryPayload` value-free — ALTERNATIVES: `DisallowUnknownFields` (refuses wider than rule 1 and breaks GET-then-PUT round-trips); a `SecretData` field on `CredentialEntryPayload` (breaks the no-response-carries-a-value guarantee); a custom `UnmarshalJSON` on `CredentialEntryPayload` (it is promoted into the embedding `credentialEntryRequest` and takes over its decode, so `SecretData` never fills); a raw-JSON presence scan per handler (a second detection mechanism).
WATCHOUT: Changing `BootstrapRequest.CredentialPools` to an unexported request type breaks package `admin_test` callers that build it with `[]admin.CredentialPoolPayload`. The implementor either exports the request type or converts inside `handleBootstrap`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap_test.go:51.
DECISION: The add and update `dryRun` branches run the step 4 checks and mutation on a copy of an unlocked `credentialPools.Get` and return via `writeDryRun`, modeled on `handleUpdateCredentialPool` — BECAUSE §15.1 **`dryRun` query parameter.** says most admin POST and PUT endpoints accept it, and a branch that skipped the checks would answer 201 where the write answers 403 — ALTERNATIVES: refusing `?dryRun=true` with 400 (contradicts §15.1); adding §15.1 "Supported endpoints:" table rows (the table is non-exhaustive); a dryRun that calls `Update` and rolls back (it performs the Put).
WATCHOUT: Do not restate in CODE-4 step 5 where the CODE-3 checks run. CODE-3 step 4 case (b) (G3) is the one home and already names these branches.
DECISION: The writer test is one sentence in Testing item 7 rather than a new numbered item — BECAUSE a new item mid-list renumbers the citers of tests 7 to 10 — ALTERNATIVES: asserting the bounded context in the test (fake clientset reactors receive the Action and not the ctx, so a fake cannot observe the deadline).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: stage the stray-Secret listing in full (SPEC-11 row plus a §24 thin-client exception item, CODE-8, Testing item 15, a TEST-1 tier 5 clause, DOCS-1 edits, a checklist step, and an index entry) rather than defer it — BECAUSE the orchestrator decision of 2026-10-30 (review-log line 757, commit 3a3a711b9) says "The proposal adds" the command, and a review-loop deferral would reverse a recorded decision and come back CONTESTED — ALTERNATIVES: defer to a follow-up and file a defect (reverses the decision); a `lenny-ctl preflight` subcommand (§17.6 **CLI equivalent:** says the CLI runs the same checks as the Job, and standalone preflight runs with no gateway, while this command needs the Admin API); a §17.6 statement (the command is not a preflight check).
DECISION: the command subtracts only refs from pools the default `GET /v1/admin/credential-pools` returns, which excludes soft-deleted pools, and sends no `includeDeleted` — BECAUSE §15.1 documents no `includeDeleted` on credential pools (the only spec hit is the tenants tombstone in §12.8), and the command is read-only, so reporting a Secret that only a soft-deleted pool names is harmless — ALTERNATIVES: `?includeDeleted=true` (code accepts it in handleListCredentialPools, but it would put an undocumented query into the §24.5 row).
DECISION: the command aborts with exit 1 and empty stdout when `credential.ParseSecretRef(ref, ns)` refuses a stored ref — BECAUSE stored refs are canonical in the gateway's credentials namespace (SPEC-2 **Secret references.** rule 2), so a refusal means `--namespace` names another namespace and every Secret would be misreported as unreferenced.
WATCHOUT: no endpoint lists credential pools across tenants. `authorizedTenantForUser` refuses a platform-admin request without `tenantId`, so the command must page `/v1/admin/tenants` and then each tenant's pools. A partial inventory reports referenced Secrets as unreferenced, which is why any failed call aborts before anything prints. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users.go:107-111; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go:542-570
FACT: pkg/ctlcli already has the two seams the command needs: `listAllAdmin[T]` pages any §15.1 cursor collection (pkg/ctlcli/main.go:2425), and `kubeSelftestPods` builds a client from the current kubeconfig with `ctrl.GetConfig()` (pkg/ctlcli/preflight_supervisor.go:255). The §24.2 `preflight supervisor` row sets the precedent for a "through the current `kubeconfig` context" API Mapping cell and a "Kubernetes permission" Min Role cell. EVIDENCE: spec/24_lenny-ctl-command-reference.md §24.2 table
WATCHOUT: use the `k8s.io/client-go/metadata` client (PartialObjectMetadataList) for the Secret list. RBAC `list` cannot be limited to metadata, but the metadata client keeps Secret values out of the CLI process and its output.

The summary's Open decisions section records this as the one open decision ... The loop derived no recommendation." is false. The orchestrator decided it on 2026-10-30, and it is staged as SPEC-11's listing row and CODE-8. Delete the bullet. status.md is outside the fixer's grant.
OPEN: docs/operator-guide/lenny-ctl.md:10 says every `lenny-ctl` operation "maps to an Admin API call". That was already false before this proposal (standalone preflight, install, up/down). It is a candidate separate docs finding and is not part of this fix.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]

- FACT: The Token Service clientset from `inClusterClientset` (cmd/lenny-token-service/main.go) has exactly one consumer, `secretprobe.New` in cmd/lenny-token-service/surfaces.go:79-82. It sets no QPS, Burst, or Timeout, and `secretprobe.Prober.ProbeSecretAccess` passes the caller ctx straight to client-go. The gateway stub (cmd/lenny-gateway/secret_probe.go) sets no gRPC deadline either. "Keep the existing bounded context" referred to nothing. EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/secretprobe/secretprobe.go:53,69,82; cmd/lenny-token-service/main.go:83-89
- FACT: The tokenservice.Metrics implementers are `NoMetrics` (pkg/tokenservice/metrics.go), `promemit.Emitter`, and the `recordingMetrics` fake (pkg/tokenservice/audit_test.go:58). `gatewaymetrics.Metrics` and `recordingRevocationObserver` share one method name but do not implement the interface. EVIDENCE: grep `func (.*) Inc5xx`
- FACT: The gateway's `--cluster-qps`/`--cluster-burst` (env LENNY_CLUSTER_QPS/LENNY_CLUSTER_BURST, defaults 100/200) are pinned by `tests/tier1_unit/flagdefaults/flagdefaults_test.go` requiredFlags. No chart template or spec section names them. The current VCS reader rides the gateway cluster client (cmd/lenny-gateway/auxstores.go:78-81), so CODE-6 moves VCS reads from a 100/200 per-gateway-replica limiter to a client-go default 5/10 per Token Service replica unless CODE-2 sets the limits. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:875-878; cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:171-172
- DECISION: The CODE-2 step 7 flags reuse the gateway's names and defaults (`--cluster-qps`/`--cluster-burst`, 100/200) — BECAUSE one spelling across binaries, and the existing flagdefaults gate takes two rows — ALTERNATIVES: `--kube-qps`/`--kube-burst` (a second spelling for the same knob); a chart value (the gateway has none either).
- DECISION: Bound the Get inside `secretprobe` with `context.WithTimeout` at a package constant (5 s) — BECAUSE the prober is the one seam both consumers (admin probe, VCS read) pass through, and a hanging httptest kube-apiserver tests it — ALTERNATIVES: `rest.Config.Timeout` in `inClusterClientset` (main.go reads in-cluster env, so no test can reach it without splitting the helper); a gateway-side gRPC deadline (that is a separate, pre-existing gap on every Token Service RPC, not this finding).
- DECISION: The secret-read outcome is recorded in `grpc.go` through `s.metrics` (a new `tokenservice.Metrics` method). The prober does not record it — BECAUSE only the handler sees the verdict, the Go error, and the nil-prober case together, and it already records through `s.metrics` in `observe`.
- WATCHOUT: client-go's fake clientset ignores ctx, so a "timeout" case over the fake clientset blocks forever or passes vacuously. The Testing item 2 timeout case needs a real clientset against an httptest server that never answers. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Testing item 2
- WATCHOUT: Fixtures that move to bare names are credential-pool `secretRef`s submitted through the admin API or the bootstrap seed, including tests/tier4_integration/drift_gatewayreader_http_test.go:131, where a seed parse error drops the pool and the :223 Fatalf fires. Connector `clientSecretRef: lenny-system/...` fixtures stay (Settled: Out of scope Secrets). Store-seeded struct literals (credentialpoolstore tests, tier-4 credential_lifecycle and revocation_propagation) do not go through `ParseSecretRef` on the write path.
- UNVERIFIED: whether any store-seeded `lenny-system/` ref later reaches the CODE-6 `Resolve` (which parses the stored ref and would now return ErrNoUsableCredential) in an existing tier-4 test. The implementor of CODE-6 should run tier 4.
- OPEN: The gateway sets no deadline on any Token Service gRPC call (cmd/lenny-gateway/secret_probe.go, executor_credentials.go). This predates 0112 and is a separate finding, not part of G2.
- The review-log DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md] asking for a Token Service QPS/burst (review-log.md:127) is discharged by this design once CODE-2 step 7 lands. Compaction can close it. status.md "Findings for the non-spec loop" QPS bullet is deleted in the same edit.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]

DECISION: The admin probe moves to where the registration check decides the introduced set: inside the `credentialPools.Update` closure against the locked row, after CODE-3 steps 4 and 6. The step 5 "ref the step 3 probe did not cover" sentinel and its 412 are deleted, and step 5 keeps only the locked `If-Match` re-check (pool PUT, and pool DELETE via CODE-4 step 6). BECAUSE the uncovered-ref case only exists because the probe set and the registration set were computed against different rows; computing both on one row removes the case instead of mapping it to a code. A Token Service call under the row lock is acceptable because CODE-4 already calls `Writer.Put` under the same lock, only admin handlers and bootstrap take that lock, and the probe fires only for a platform-admin introduction. ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's re-probe-and-retry-once (needs a terminal outcome when the retry also finds an uncovered ref, so it is a loop); in-closure probe of only the uncovered residue (two probe sites plus the "did not cover" concept); a different status code such as 409 or 503 (invents error semantics §15.1 does not define).
DECISION: `handleCreateCredentialPool` has no closure and no stored row (INSERT on a soft-deleted name fails 23505 → 409), so it runs steps 4, 6, and 3 in the handler, ahead of its `dryRun` branch and `Create`, with every submitted ref introduced. The pool PUT `dryRun` branch runs the same steps against its unlocked read. Both sit in one placement sentence in CODE-3 step 4. BECAUSE step 4 "inside each Update closure" left the create POST with no 403, which fails open for a tenant-admin (finding G3-1), and moving the probe into the closure would otherwise remove the probe from `dryRun`. Running steps 4 and 6 in `dryRun` as well is a deliberate extension. A `dryRun` that returns 200 for a request the write refuses with 403 is the same gap.
FACT: `pgstore.Update` runs the closure once with no retry, and a closure error rolls back the transaction. `writeCredentialMutateError` maps any unknown error to 400, so a probe error returned from a closure needs explicit 422/503 mapping on that path and on the pool PUT error path. `probeSecretRefs` has no callers outside `credential_pools.go`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/credentialpoolstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:133-190; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go:363, :1262-1275.
WATCHOUT: Once the probe is in the closure, the review-log trap "CODE-3 step order. Step 5 must run after step 4 ... and after step 6" no longer applies, because step 5 has no introduced-set check. The ordering that matters is now that step 3 (the probe) runs after steps 4 and 6, which SPEC-2 (g) requires as "403 with no probe" and "no probe for a rule-3-refused reference". EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 steps 3-6.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G2.1 / spec.2.follow-up-fix.1, Settled **Probe mechanism.**]: "step 5 (412) runs after steps 4 and 6" is withdrawn. The probe runs inside the closure after steps 4 and 6, and no 412 exists for an introduced ref.
DECISION: CODE-3 step 7 reads "a reserved-prefix ref that step 6 refuses", and Testing item 6 reads "a prefixed ref the existing entry does not carry". BECAUSE step 6 already states the bootstrap predicate, so step 7 only points to it.
OPEN: The non-spec loop should check whether the bootstrap `opts.dryRun` path applies step 6 against its unlocked read, like the pool PUT `dryRun`. This design leaves bootstrap governed by step 7.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]

FACT: Every credential-pool write handler and the seed decode leniently (`json.NewDecoder(req.Body).Decode`, `json.Unmarshal(raw, &body)`, no `DisallowUnknownFields`), so a field absent from the target type is dropped silently. Pool create, pool PUT, and the seed decode `CredentialPoolPayload`, whose entries are `CredentialEntryPayload`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/credential_pools.go:457-458, :653-654; bootstrap.go:69, :170-171
DECISION: rule 1's pool-level `secretData` refusal is reached by decoding pool create, pool PUT, and seed `credentials` through a request-only pool type whose entries are `credentialEntryRequest` — BECAUSE it reuses the step 1 entry type and keeps the "no response type carries a value" invariant — ALTERNATIVES: `DisallowUnknownFields` (refuses every unknown field, so GET-then-PUT round-trips and older clients break; wider than rule 1); a `SecretData` field on `CredentialEntryPayload` (breaks the type-level no-value-in-responses guarantee); a custom `UnmarshalJSON` on `CredentialEntryPayload` recording presence (the method is promoted into the embedding `credentialEntryRequest` and swallows its `SecretData` decode).
WATCHOUT: `BootstrapRequest` is exported and package `admin_test` builds it with `[]admin.CredentialPoolPayload` (bootstrap_test.go:50-51 and others). Changing `BootstrapRequest.CredentialPools` to an unexported request type breaks those tests at compile time; either export the request type or decode `credentialPools` a second time from the `raw` body that `handleBootstrap` already holds. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap.go:166-171
FACT: `handleAddCredential` and `handleUpdateCredentialEntry` have no `dryRun` branch; only pool create (:506) and pool PUT (:708) do, plus the bootstrap `opts.dryRun` path. §15.1 **`dryRun` query parameter.** says "Most admin POST and PUT endpoints accept ?dryRun=true" and excepts only action endpoints and DELETE, so credential add and update are in scope of the spec today; the "Supported endpoints:" table is non-exhaustive (it omits bootstrap, which supports dryRun). EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1180, :1220-1240
DECISION: the new add/update dryRun branches follow the pool PUT one (unlocked `Get`, apply to a copy, `writeDryRun`) and run the CODE-4 step 4 checks and mutation on that copy, stopping before the writer call — BECAUSE §15.1 dryRun is full validation including policy checks, so the 403/400 of CODE-3 steps 4 and 6 and the rule 6 refusal must answer as the write would — ALTERNATIVES: refusing `dryRun` on these two endpoints (contradicts "Most admin POST and PUT endpoints accept"); adding §15.1 table rows (a spec edit this non-spec loop does not need, since the table is non-exhaustive).
OPEN: pool create and pool PUT dryRun branches run before the CODE-3 step 4 registration check (which lives in the `Update` closure), so a tenant-admin dryRun PUT introducing a ref may answer 200 where the write answers 403. Not caused or repaired by G4; a later round should check CODE-3 against §15.1 dryRun "policy checks".
FACT: client-go fake clientset reactors receive the `Action`, not the context, so the CODE-4 step 3 "bounded context" is not observable through a fake clientset; the writer test pins the verb sequence and NotFound/AlreadyExists mapping only. EVIDENCE: k8s.io/client-go/testing reactor signature
DECISION: the writer test goes into Testing item 7 as one sentence citing CODE-4 step 3, with reactors refusing `get` and `patch` as the discriminating condition — BECAUSE a new numbered item renumbers citers ("tests 7 to 10", "Testing item 6") — ALTERNATIVES: a new item 15 (orders out of deliverable order for no gain).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The probe's placement has one home, CODE-3 step 3. Its condition list gains "never in a `dryRun` branch (§15.1 **`dryRun` query parameter.**) or on the seed (step 7)". CODE-3 step 4 stops naming step 3 ("Steps 4 and 6 run ...") and drops its seed parenthetical. CODE-4 step 4 drops "(step 3 excluded on the seed closure)". BECAUSE step 4 kept enumerating where the probe runs, and each enumeration missed a case (the dryRun branches this round). With one home, a later dryRun branch inherits the exclusion without anyone editing step 4. ALTERNATIVES: adding "and in every dryRun branch" to step 4's parenthetical is the same enumeration one item longer, so it was rejected. Restating the exclusion in CODE-4 step 5 would put it in a second home.
DECISION: The S15/S16 forward references are closed by keeping CODE-3 text generic and moving the cases that depend on CODE-4 into CODE-4's tests. CODE-3 step 4 drops "(pool PUT, credential add, and credential update; CODE-4 step 5)". Testing item 5 keeps the pool PUT 412 case. The pool DELETE 412/no-writer case moves to item 8, and the tenant-admin secretData case moves to item 7. Item 8's dryRun sentence also points at the item 5 dryRun cases for the new add/update branches. The checklist is unchanged. BECAUSE CODE-4 step 4 runs CODE-3's checks inside its closures, so S16 cannot move ahead of S15. Credential add and update already run `credentialPools.Update` closures today (credential_pools.go handleAddCredential/handleUpdateCredentialEntry), so CODE-3 step 4 can land at S15. ALTERNATIVES: flipping S15/S16 fails on that dependency. Merging S15 and S16 into one step would renumber S17 to S24 and every Depends-on list. Tagging item 5 "(CODE-3, CODE-4)" leaves it unclear which step lands the test.
WATCHOUT: The dryRun branches on credential add and update are CODE-4 additions, and the §15.1 dryRun supported-endpoints table lists only POST/PUT `/v1/admin/credential-pools`. Do not cite the add/update dryRun branches from CODE-3. OPEN: CODE-3 step 7 records `seedValidationCode` for a `secretData` field, and Testing item 6 asserts it, both at S15. The seed decodes `credentials` into `CredentialEntryPayload` (bootstrap.go:69 via CredentialPoolPayload), which drops `secretData` until CODE-4 step 1 adds the request-only type at S16. This is the same S15-to-S16 forward reference, filed for a later round.

FACT: The Token Service `--secret-namespace` default is `POD_NAMESPACE`, falling back to `lenny-system`. The gateway's new `--credentials-namespace` default is `lenny-credentials`. Only the chart launches the Token Service, and CHART-1 step 4 renders both flags from one helper, so the mismatch in defaults has no effect on any shipped path. With no chart grant in the release namespace, a read there fails as DENIED. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-token-service/flags.go:128
FACT: The gateway HTTP layer has no request-body capture middleware, so SPEC-2 rule 7's logging exclusion needs no new route list. ### [non-spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-4 step 1 applies SPEC-2 (f) rule 1 "after decode, and on the seed in the per-entry checks of `upsertCredentialPools` (`seedValidationCode`)", replacing "at decode" — BECAUSE the seed's decode is the single whole-body `json.Unmarshal` of `BootstrapRequest` in `handleBootstrap`, whose failure is a request-level 400 that aborts every seed section; every other seed refusal is `out.add(i, p.Name, actionError, seedValidationCode, ...)` — ALTERNATIVES: reinstating the clause in CODE-3 step 7 (S15 forward reference to the S16 request type, reverses round 4); restating rule 1's 400 and per-entry outcome in CODE-4 (rule 1 owns them); deleting "at decode" with no seed site (no code step would name the per-entry code).
WATCHOUT: Do not implement the seed `secretData` refusal as a strict decoder or custom `UnmarshalJSON` that errors on the field. On the seed that is a request-level 400 for the whole bootstrap body. Testing item 7's per-entry `seedValidationCode` assertion is what discriminates it — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap.go handleBootstrap `json.Unmarshal(raw, &body)` arm; bootstrap_resources.go `seedValidationCode` calls.
DECISION: Testing item 6's seed sentence now covers canonical storage of a bare seed ref and per-entry `seedValidationCode` for a foreign-namespace ref, an invalid name, and a prefixed ref, "with the other entries applied" — BECAUSE SPEC-2 **Secret references.** rule 2 applies canonicalization and per-entry refusal on the seed, and the trailing clause discriminates a request-level abort — ALTERNATIVES: widening item 4 to the seed (its 422, 503, and details.field assertions do not apply to the seed, which runs no probe).
DECISION: Testing item 7 runs rule 1's last sentence on an empty `secretData`, an invalid key, and values totalling one byte over `corev1.MaxSecretSize` with the at-limit case passing — BECAUSE one case per sentence cannot cover the three refusals, and a missing size check would let an oversize value reach the writer and map to 503 — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "each with no writer call" (item 8's rule 5 4xx case already owns that).
FACT: Kubernetes Secret validation sums value bytes only (keys excluded) and refuses only when the total exceeds `MaxSecretSize` (1 MiB), so data exactly at the limit is accepted even though the types.go doc comment says "less than" — EVIDENCE: k8s.io/api@v0.31.4/core/v1/types.go:7201,7237. An implementation that adds key bytes would fail item 7's at-limit case.
USEFUL [non-spec.5 WATCHOUT on bootstrap.go:40-42 seedValidationCode]: confirmed the per-entry code without re-deriving it.

### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]

DECISION: the seed `secretData` refusal stays homed in CODE-4 step 1 (S16), and "at decode" is replaced by the site: the pool handlers after decode, and the per-entry checks of `upsertCredentialPools` with `seedValidationCode` — BECAUSE the seed's decode is one whole-body `json.Unmarshal` in `handleBootstrap`, so a decode-time refusal is a request-level 400 that aborts every section — ALTERNATIVES: moving it back to CODE-3 step 7 (rejected: forward reference to the S16 request type, round 4); restating rule 1's 400 and per-entry outcomes in CODE-4 (rejected: SPEC-2 rule 1 is their home).
WATCHOUT: do not write a "refuse at decode" or a custom `UnmarshalJSON` that returns an error for `secretData` on any type the bootstrap body embeds; it turns a per-entry error into a request-level 400. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/bootstrap.go:170-174 (whole-body Unmarshal -> 400), bootstrap.go:340-352 (per-entry `seedValidationCode` checks before the store read).
FACT: Kubernetes Secret validation counts only value bytes against `corev1.MaxSecretSize` (1 MiB, k8s.io/api core/v1 types.go) and refuses only `> MaxSecretSize`, so values totalling exactly the limit pass. CODE-4 step 1's "on the total" does not say whether key bytes count; the at-limit test case pins values-only.
DECISION: the seed rule 2 test (canonical storage, rule 1 and rule 3 per-entry refusals) replaces the last sentence of Testing item 6 rather than extending item 4 — BECAUSE item 4's 422 and 503 assertions do not apply to the seed, which runs no probe; "with the other entries applied" is the assertion that discriminates a request-level abort.
